1→"""Project policy — persistent project-specific rules for triage and review."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→from typing import Any
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    13→
    14→
    15→def _default_policy_path() -> Path:
    16→    return get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / "project_policy.json"
    17→
    18→
    19→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    20→class PolicyLoadResult:
    21→    ok: bool
    22→    policy: dict[str, Any]
    23→    message: str = ""
    24→    error_kind: str | None = None
    25→
    26→
    27→def load_policy_result(path: Path | None = None) -> PolicyLoadResult:
    28→    p = path or _default_policy_path()
    29→    if not p.exists():
    30→        return PolicyLoadResult(ok=True, policy={"rules": []})
    31→    try:
    32→        data = json.loads(p.read_text())
    33→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    34→        return PolicyLoadResult(
    35→            ok=False,
    36→            policy={"rules": []},
    37→            message=str(exc),
    38→            error_kind="policy_parse_error",
    39→        )
    40→    except OSError as exc:
    41→        return PolicyLoadResult(
    42→            ok=False,
    43→            policy={"rules": []},
    44→            message=str(exc),
    45→            error_kind="policy_read_error",
    46→        )
    47→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    48→        return PolicyLoadResult(
    49→            ok=False,
    50→            policy={"rules": []},
    51→            message="project policy is not a JSON object",
    52→            error_kind="policy_invalid_shape",
    53→        )
    54→    data.setdefault("rules", [])
    55→    if not isinstance(data.get("rules"), list):
    56→        return PolicyLoadResult(
    57→            ok=False,
    58→            policy={"rules": []},
    59→            message="project policy 'rules' must be a list",
    60→            error_kind="policy_invalid_rules",
    61→        )
    62→    return PolicyLoadResult(ok=True, policy=data)
    63→
    64→
    65→def load_policy(path: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
    66→    return load_policy_result(path).policy
    67→
    68→
    69→def save_policy(policy: dict[str, Any], path: Path | None = None) -> None:
    70→    p = path or _default_policy_path()
    71→    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    72→    safe_write_text(p, json.dumps(policy, indent=2) + "\n")
    73→
    74→
    75→def add_rule(policy: dict[str, Any], text: str) -> int:
    76→    """Add a rule, return its 1-based index."""
    77→    rules = policy.setdefault("rules", [])
    78→    rules.append({"text": text, "created_at": utc_now()})
    79→    return len(rules)
    80→
    81→
    82→def remove_rule(policy: dict[str, Any], index: int) -> str | None:
    83→    """Remove a rule by 1-based index, return its text or None."""
    84→    rules = policy.get("rules", [])
    85→    if 1 <= index <= len(rules):
    86→        return rules.pop(index - 1)["text"]
    87→    return None
    88→
    89→
    90→def render_policy_block(policy: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    91→    """Render rules as a prompt section. Returns empty string if no rules."""
    92→    rules = policy.get("rules", [])
    93→    if not rules:
    94→        return ""
    95→    lines = [
    96→        "## Project Policy\n",
    97→        "The following project-specific rules MUST be respected.",
    98→        "Do NOT suggest or implement changes that violate these rules.",
    99→        "Flag any action step or suggestion that would violate them.\n",
   100→    ]
   101→    for i, rule in enumerate(rules, 1):
   102→        lines.append(f"{i}. {rule['text']}")
   103→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
   104→
   105→
   106→__all__ = [
   107→    "add_rule",
   108→    "load_policy",
   109→    "load_policy_result",
   110→    "PolicyLoadResult",
   111→    "remove_rule",
   112→    "render_policy_block",
   113→    "save_policy",
   114→]
   115→

    11→import desloppify.intelligence.narrative.core as narrative_mod
    12→from desloppify.base.coercions import coerce_positive_int
    13→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS
    14→from desloppify.engine._plan.persistence import plan_path_for_state
    15→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel

    17→    HolisticReviewPrepareOptions,
    18→    prepare_holistic_review,
    19→)
    20→
    21→from .. import runner_packets as runner_packets_mod
    22→from ..helpers import parse_dimensions
    23→from ..runtime_paths import (
    24→    blind_packet_path,

    26→    runtime_project_root,
    27→)
    28→from .policy import coerce_review_batch_file_limit, redacted_review_config
    29→
    30→
    31→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    32→class ReviewPacketContext:
    33→    """Normalized review-packet CLI options shared across entrypoints."""
    34→
    35→    path: Path
    36→    state_path: Path | None
    37→    dimensions: list[str] | None
    38→    retrospective: bool
    39→    retrospective_max_issues: int
    40→    retrospective_max_batch_items: int
    41→
    42→
    43→def resolve_review_packet_context(args: Any) -> ReviewPacketContext:
    44→    """Parse shared packet options from CLI args."""
    45→    dims = parse_dimensions(args)
    46→    dimensions = list(dims) if dims else None
    47→    retrospective = bool(getattr(args, "retrospective", True))
    48→    retrospective_max_issues = coerce_positive_int(
    49→        getattr(args, "retrospective_max_issues", None),
    50→        default=30,
    51→        minimum=1,
    52→    )
    53→    retrospective_max_batch_items = coerce_positive_int(
    54→        getattr(args, "retrospective_max_batch_items", None),
    55→        default=20,
    56→        minimum=1,
    57→    )
    58→    return ReviewPacketContext(
    59→        path=Path(getattr(args, "path", ".") or "."),
    60→        state_path=(
    61→            Path(getattr(args, "state"))
    62→            if getattr(args, "state", None)
    63→            else None
    64→        ),
    65→        dimensions=dimensions,
    66→        retrospective=retrospective,
    67→        retrospective_max_issues=retrospective_max_issues,
    68→        retrospective_max_batch_items=retrospective_max_batch_items,
    69→    )
    70→
    71→
    72→def build_holistic_packet(
    73→    *,
    74→    state: StateModel,
    75→    lang: Any,
    76→    config: dict[str, Any],
    77→    context: ReviewPacketContext,
    78→    setup_lang_fn,
    79→    prepare_holistic_review_fn=None,
    80→) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], str]:
    81→    """Build the canonical holistic review packet payload and lang name."""
    82→    lang_run, found_files = setup_lang_fn(lang, context.path, config)
    83→    lang_name = lang_run.name
    84→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
    85→        state,
    86→        context=narrative_mod.NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="review"),
    87→    )
    88→    prepare_fn = prepare_holistic_review_fn or prepare_holistic_review
    89→    packet = prepare_fn(
    90→        context.path,
    91→        lang_run,
    92→        state,
    93→        options=HolisticReviewPrepareOptions(
    94→            dimensions=context.dimensions,
    95→            files=found_files or None,
    96→            max_files_per_batch=coerce_review_batch_file_limit(config),
    97→            include_issue_history=context.retrospective,
    98→            issue_history_max_issues=context.retrospective_max_issues,
    99→            issue_history_max_batch_items=context.retrospective_max_batch_items,
   100→        ),

    22→def _load_json_payload_from_path(path: Path) -> dict:
    23→    """Load a JSON payload from *path* and return a dict."""

    43→
    44→def _override_filename(filename: str) -> str:
    45→    """Convert ``foo.json`` to ``foo.override.json``."""
    46→    if not filename.endswith(".json"):
    47→        return f"{filename}.override.json"
    48→    return f"{filename[:-5]}.override.json"

    50→

    53→    return _load_json_payload_from_path(_DATA_DIR / filename)

    56→def _validate_optional_string_list(value: object, *, context: str) -> list[str]:
    57→    """Validate an optional list of strings (empty allowed)."""
    58→    if value is None:
    59→        return []
    60→    if not isinstance(value, list):
    61→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a list of strings")
    62→    out: list[str] = []
    63→    for idx, item in enumerate(value):
    64→        if not isinstance(item, str) or not item.strip():
    65→            raise ValueError(f"{context}[{idx}] must be a non-empty string")
    66→        out.append(item)
    67→    return out

    69→
    70→def _apply_dimensions_override(
    71→    base_payload: dict,
    72→    override_payload: dict,

    87→        "system_prompt",
    88→        "system_prompt_append",

    94→

   113→    )
   114→    if remove_dims:

   150→def _load_payload_for_lang(
   151→    lang_name: str,

   158→    Resolution order:

   160→    2) If language override payload exists, patch the selected base.

   171→            dims_key=dims_key,
   172→            context=str(lang_override_path),
   173→        )
   174→        context = f"{context} + {lang_override_path}"
   175→
   176→    return base_payload, context

## Summary: Reviewer Launch Prompt Assembly

Based on my analysis of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py` (lines 190-330) and the supporting infrastructure, here's how the system prompt and policy block are combined:

### Prompt Assembly Architecture

The launch prompt is built via `_build_claude_launch_prompt()` in `external.py` using a **join-non-empty-sections pattern** (lines 317-327):

```python
return join_non_empty_sections(
    header,
    *batch_sections,
    policy_text,
    render_scoring_frame(),
    render_scan_evidence_note(),
    render_task_requirements(issues_cap=combined_cap, dim_set=all_dims),
    render_scope_enums(),
    output_schema,
    session_requirements,
)
```

**Order of sections in the final prompt:**
1. **Header** — Session metadata (id, token, file paths)
2. **Batch sections** (multiple) — Each batch contains dimension prompts, context, findings, concerns
3. **Policy block** (line 320) — Project policy rules
4. **Scoring frame** — General task instruction
5. **Scan evidence note** — Navigation guide for mechanical findings
6. **Task requirements** — 2-phase workflow (Observe/Judge) with numbered steps
7. **Scope enums** — Allowed values for impact_scope/fix_scope
8. **Output schema** — JSON structure expected from reviewer
9. **Session requirements** — Session ID/token preservation rules

### System Prompt Location

The **system prompt is NOT in the launch prompt text**. Instead, it's stored in the blind packet JSON:

- **Loaded at**: `desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_holistic_orchestration.py:194`
- **Source**: `load_dimensions_for_lang(lang.name)` → returns `(dims, prompts, system_prompt)` 
- **Stored in packet**: `payload["system_prompt"]` (line 194)
- **Referenced in instructions**: Line 563 of `prompt_sections.py` tells reviewer: *"1. Read the blind packet's `system_prompt` — scoring rules and calibration."*

The system_prompt defines **scoring rules and calibration** and lives alongside the investigation batches in the blind packet JSON file.

### Policy Block Assembly

The policy block is rendered independently (lines 305-308):

```python
policy_result = load_policy_result()  # Load from .desloppify/project_policy.json
policy_text = render_policy_block(policy_result.policy)
```

**Location of policy rendering**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/policy/project.py:90-103`

The `render_policy_block()` function:
- Returns empty string if no rules exist
- Creates a titled section: `"## Project Policy\n"` + `"The following project-specific rules MUST be respected..."`
- Numbered list of rules from `policy["rules"]` list

### Key Injection Points for Execution Constraints

For injecting execution constraints, you have **three strategic locations**:

1. **System prompt override** — Modify the system_prompt loaded from dimensions data (affects all reviewers):
   - File: `desloppify/languages/_framework/review_data/dimensions.json`
   - Or per-language override: `desloppify/languages/{lang}/review_data/dimensions.override.json`
   - Loaded via `load_dimensions_for_lang()` → packaged at `prepare_holistic_orchestration.py:194`

2. **Policy rules** — Add to project policy file (most user-facing approach):
   - File: `.desloppify/project_policy.json`
   - Loaded via `load_policy_result()` (project.py:27-62)
   - Rendered by `render_policy_block()` (project.py:90-103)
   - Inserted in launch prompt at line 320

3. **Task requirements section** — Modify `render_task_requirements()` in `prompt_sections.py:559-600`
   - This section defines the 2-phase workflow and numbered instruction steps
   - Can add execution constraints as additional numbered steps

The policy block appears **after batch sections but BEFORE** the scoring frame and output schema, making it a highly visible constraint location when the reviewer reads the prompt sequentially.
